# The text-only functional errata records, checked against period rulings (round 037)

Round 037 checks every erratum record whose `functional` transition applies at Edison's (2010-04-24) or Tengu's (2011-09-17) snapshot,
and that no ruling was ever asked about, against the period rulings in the corpus, then acts on what those rulings say. It follows
rounds 035 and 036 (`period-rulings-generated-scripts.md`) and the owner's decision of 2026-09-29 (`docs/state.md`, "Period rulings").
This document is the record of the search and the verdicts. **No suite run is evidence for anything in it**: every claim is a passage read at an archived primary document.

## 1. Which records

The brief's four conditions, re-derived from the raw `data/errata/*.json` (`tests/test_errata_rulings_gate.py` re-derives them again, independently of the validator, on every run):
a `functional` transition; citing no source with a `ruling_class` in `data/sources.json`; whose event takes effect after Edison's snapshot; on a record no generated card implements.

| group (by the record's coverage) | records |
|---|---|
| reuse-upstream | 61 |
| known-gap | 19 |
| none-needed | 3 |
| **total** | **83** |

That is the brief's 83 (61, 19 and 3). Two notes on the count. A record whose coverage is `reuse-upstream` in any state is counted in that group, as the brief does; a few of them ship a variant only at one snapshot or in one state.
The gate the validator enforces is per transition, not per record (`Validator._check_erratum_rulings`): a `functional` transition that is not yet in effect at Edison's snapshot, or cannot be placed against it, needs a check. That covers 8 records the four conditions leave out, and this round checked them too, marked "extra" below:

- **Anteatereatingant** (`anteatereatingant.json`): event undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.
- **Armored Glass** (`armored-glass.json`): event undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.
- **Curse of Royal** (`curse-of-royal.json`): event undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.
- **Dimension Distortion** (`dimension-distortion.json`): c1 undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.
- **Necrovalley** (`necrovalley.json`): c1 2010-10-15 (Edison old, Tengu new); c2 2012-09-29 (Edison old, Tengu old); c3 2017-03-30 (Edison old, Tengu old). a later functional change after an earlier one that took effect before Edison's snapshot (condition 3 reads the first).
- **Spirit's Invitation** (`spirit-s-invitation.json`): c1 undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.
- **Swords of Concealing Light** (`swords-of-concealing-light.json`): c2 2012-09-29 (Edison old, Tengu old); c3 2017-04-13 (Edison old, Tengu old). a later functional change after an earlier one that took effect before Edison's snapshot (condition 3 reads the first).
- **YZ-Tank Dragon** (`yz-tank-dragon.json`): c1 undated (Edison ambiguous, Tengu ambiguous). undated event: its state at both snapshots is `ambiguous`.

GOAT-only records are out of scope. A transition that already cites a ruling source (Dark Necrofear's, Fushioh Richie's and Second Coin Toss's) is not asked again; a record a generated card implements is covered by the card's own check.
The order of work was reuse-upstream, then known-gap, then none-needed, then the extras; **all four groups were done completely.**

## 2. The corpus and how it was searched

Every document below was fetched from its archived capture (`data/sources.json`), converted to text, and searched by the card's name (curly quotes and dashes normalised) and by the words of each claimed difference. A document counts at a snapshot only if it existed then:

| document | in the corpus as | at Edison | at Tengu |
|---|---|---|---|
| Konami-hosted UDE card FAQ, 2008-12 (pages A-C, D-E, F-H, I-K, L-O, P-R, S-T, U-Z) and UDE's own U-Z page of 2009-02-26 | `konami-card-faq-*`, `ude-card-faq-2009-02-26-uz` | yes | yes |
| UDE card rulings page, 2005 | `ude-card-rulings-archive` | yes | yes |
| archived UDE judge-list (Netrep) threads reached from Yugipedia's `Card Rulings:` pages | `ude-judge-list-*` | yes | yes |
| Konami's errata lists 2009-07-30, 2010-01-05 / 2010-11-05 | `konami-errata-list-*` | first two | all three |
| Konami's rulebooks 7.0 (2008), 7.1 (2010-03-30) / 7.2 (2011-05), 8.0 (2011-09) | `konami-*rulebook*` | 7.0, 7.1 | all four |
| Konami per-set ruling documents: Crimson Crisis, Raging Battle, Ancient Prophecy, Stardust Overdrive, Absolute Powerforce v1.0, Duelist Pack Collection Tin 2010, Machina Mayhem | `konami-*-rulings-*` | yes | yes |
| The Shining Darkness (2010-04-30), Starstrike Blast, Storm of Ragnarok, Extreme Victory, Duelist Revolution, Absolute Powerforce v1.1, Hidden Arsenal 3, Gold Series 3 | `konami-*-rulings-*` | no | yes |

Yugipedia's `Card Rulings:<name>` page for each card was read only as a pointer (`yugipedia-card-rulings-pointer`): most TCG entries link the OCG database, which is not a TCG ruling; the archived UDE threads and Konami documents it links were read as primary documents. **Limits of the search:** the corpus is the archived pages that exist; the UDE forums are gone and only the threads Yugipedia links, and nine of them, could be read; a name the corpus spells differently ("D. D. Scout Plane", a dash in "Blackwing - Sirocco the Dawn") was found by a punctuation-insensitive search after the first pass missed it; and **absence proves nothing**: a card the corpus does not mention is `unresolved`, not "no difference".

## 3. Verdicts

A verdict is per claimed difference and per snapshot. `supported`: a ruling in force says the card played the way the record claims. `contradicted`: a ruling in force says it played as the modern card does. `unresolved`: nothing in the corpus addresses it. A UDE-era ruling counts at both snapshots unless a later Konami document replaced it (`in_force: by-decision`, the owner's decision); a Konami document counts from its date. Three refinements this round applied, each keeping a verdict honest:

- A `supported` verdict that rests on a general rule or another card's ruling, not on a passage about the card, is recorded `unresolved (general rule only)`, and the passage is kept as `does-not-address`.
- A `contradicted` verdict that rests on an inference (a passage plus a rule) is `unresolved (inference only)`; no data changes for it and it is on the owner's list.
- A contradiction that another period source in the same entry contradicts is `unresolved (period sources conflict)`; the contradicting finding is recorded with its range in force `not-shown` (a tracked warning), not applied.

A class ruling that Konami states for the class the card belongs to (the Union limit, the "except" wording, the Damage Step limit) counts as a ruling on the card, as in round 035.

## 4. Counts

Differences counted per record (a record can carry several); the verdict is the same at Edison and Tengu for every difference in this round except Necrovalley's c1, which applies at Edison only.

| group | records checked | transitions made cosmetic | narrowed | differences supported | contradicted | unresolved |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| reuse-upstream | 61 | 5 | 1 | 48 | 9 | 64 |
| known-gap | 19 | 5 | 6 | 2 | 15 | 28 |
| none-needed | 3 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 1 |
| extra | 8 | 0 | 1 | 5 | 2 | 14 |
| **total** | **91** | **12** | **8** | **55** | **28** | **107** |

## 5. Every record

One entry per record: the claimed difference or differences with the verdict at Edison / Tengu, the passages read (the quote and where), and the action. `V:` is what the substituted Ignis variant script does differently from Ignis's modern script, from a `diff` of the two at the pinned CardScripts revision (reuse-upstream records; what the list ships).

### reuse-upstream

#### Ancient Fairy Dragon (`ancient-fairy-dragon.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: narrowed**.

- **d1** (unresolved): Era: each of the two effects is a plain 'Once per turn' (a separate limit for each copy of the card, so two copies each get a use). Modern: each effect is a hard once per turn shared by name ('You can only use each effect of "Ancient Fairy Dragon" once per turn').
  - V: Variant uses SetCountLimit(1) on both effects (per copy); modern script uses SetCountLimit(1,{id,0}) and SetCountLimit(1,{id,1}) (per name). The variant implements the era per-copy limit.
  - why: Nothing in the corpus says how a plain 'once per turn' effect on Ancient Fairy Dragon (or plain once-per-turn effects in general) counts across two copies; the ANPR entry only repeats the printed 'Once per turn'. At Tengu: Same as Edison: no Tengu-only document (rulebooks v7.2/v8, later set PDFs) addresses per-copy versus per-name once-per-turn counting.
  - does-not-address, Konami "ANCIENT PROPHECY SNEAK PEEK – CARD RULINGS Compiled as of August 11, 2009 - <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture) (2009): "Once per turn, you can Special Summon 1 Level 4 or lower monster from your hand."
- **d2** (contradicted): Modern destroys 'as many cards in the Field Zones as possible (min. 1)'; the record treats this as widened from 'Field Spells on the field'. In the era only Field Spells occupied the Field Zone, so the era and modern destroy the same set of cards.
  - why: The ANPR ruling says the effect destroys both Field Spells when there are two, and the rulebook puts Field Spell Cards in the Field Zone: the era effect already destroyed every card in the Field Zones, exactly what the modern wording describes. At Tengu: Same: ANPR and rulebooks v7.1/v7.2/v8 all in force; nothing later changes the point.
  - contradicts, Konami "ANCIENT PROPHECY SNEAK PEEK – CARD RULINGS Compiled as of August 11, 2009 - <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture) (2009): "If there are 2 Field Spell Cards on the field the effect of "Ancient Fairy Dragon" will destroy both of them."
  - contradicts, Konami "ANCIENT PROPHECY SNEAK PEEK – CARD RULINGS Compiled as of August 11, 2009 - <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture) (2009): "The effect of "Ancient Fairy Dragon" can destroy Set Field Spell Cards."
  - supports, Konami "Official Rulebook" Version 7.0 (archived 2008-12-30) (2008): "Special Spell Cards called Field Spell Cards are played here."
- **d3** (unresolved): Modern: the follow-up search may not add a Field Spell sharing a name with a destroyed card ('with a different name than the destroyed card(s)'). Era: any Field Spell from the Deck may be added.
  - V: Variant thfilter checks only IsFieldSpell and IsAbleToHand (no name exclusion); modern thfilter excludes any code in Duel.GetOperatedGroup(). The variant implements the era printed wording.
  - why: The ANPR entry repeats the printed 'add 1 Field Spell Card from your Deck' with no name restriction; that is printed text, not a ruling on whether a same-name Field Spell could be added, and no other entry addresses it. At Tengu: Same as Edison.
  - does-not-address, Konami "ANCIENT PROPHECY SNEAK PEEK – CARD RULINGS Compiled as of August 11, 2009 - <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture) (2009): "If you activate the effect of "Ancient Fairy Dragon" to destroy a Field Spell Card, you must both destroy a Field Spell Card"
- searched: Card FAQ (Konami-hosted UDE FAQ pages 2008-12, near-Edison captures): Ancient Fairy Dragon; once per turn per copy / two copies; Field Spell destruction → No Ancient Fairy Dragon entry (card released after the FAQ). Copy-counting hits (Boss Rus…; 2005 UDE card rulings / UDE FAQ 2009-02-26 U-Z: Ancient Fairy Dragon, Field Spell destruction, once-per-turn copies → Nothing on the card.; Errata lists 2009-07-30, 2010-01-05, 2010-11-05: Ancient Fairy Dragon; Field Spell wording → Card not listed.; Rulebooks v7.0, v7.1, v7.2, v8: Field Zone contents; once-per-turn effect rule for multiple copies → Field Zone holds Field Spell Cards, one active at a time. Only once-per-turn mentions are…; Per-set PDFs (ANPR, SOVR, CRMS, RGBT, ABPF, HA, Machina Mayhem, TSHD, EXVC, STOR, STBL, DREV, HA3, Gold Series 3): Ancient Fairy Dragon; same-name search restriction; per-copy once per turn → Only the ANPR entry (destroy does not target; must destroy and gain LP to search; destroy…; UDE judge-list threads (Zorc, Snake Deity, Green Baboon 2007): once per turn counting → Zorc thread covers one card's effect used twice in a turn, not copies.

#### Armored Cybern (`armored-cybern.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: 'A monster can only be equipped with 1 Union monster at a time' is a Union condition, so a monster carries only one Union at a time. Modern: the condition is gone and a monster may carry several Unions.
  - V: Variant calls aux.AddUnionProcedure(c,s.filter,true); the true (oldequip) flag limits a monster to one equipped Union at a time. Modern script omits the flag.
  - why: The UDE FAQ (2008-12) states as a general point that a monster can only be equipped with 1 Union Monster at a time and that this is a condition (survives negation), and the Collected Power entry has surplus Unions destroyed. Armored Cybern's own entry is silent, so this is the general Union rule, not a card-specific r… At Tengu: Same FAQ still in force; the Tengu-only Shining Darkness Delta Tri ruling ('cannot activate the effect to equip a Union monster if Delta Tri is already equipped with a Union monster') is consistent and no document lifts the limit.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "The Machine-Type monster can still only be equipped with 1 Union Monster at a time, because that is a condition, not an effect"
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If multiple Union Monsters are going to be equipped to 1 legal target monster by "Collected Power", you select 1 of them and equip it to the target monster, and the other Union Monster Cards are destroyed."
  - supports, Konami "THE SHINING DARKNESS SNEAK PEEK - CARD RULINGS, Compiled as of April 30, 2010 <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture 2010-06-01) (2010-04-30): "You cannot activate the effect to equip a Union monster if Delta Tri is already equipped with a Union monster."
- **d2** (unresolved (general rule only)): Era: the equipped monster's replacement ('destroy this card instead') applies only to destruction by battle. Modern: 'destroyed by battle or card effect'.
  - V: With oldequip=true, Auxiliary.UnionReplace limits the substitution to REASON_BATTLE; the modern script's substitution covers effect destruction too.
  - why: The UDE FAQ says Heavy Mech Support Platform is different from other Union Monsters in applying its substitution to any destruction, not just battle, and that this does not apply to previous Union Monsters (Armored Cybern is a 2008 Union). Thin: no entry names Armored Cybern's substitution, and the 2010 Machina Mayhem… At Tengu: Same FAQ still in force; no Konami document up to Tengu (rulebooks v7.2/v8, later set PDFs, errata lists) changes it or rules on effect destruction of a Union's equipped monster.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "Note that "Heavy Mech Support Platform" is different from other Union Monsters in that its last sentence is applied if the monster it is equipped to is destroyed (period), not just when the monster is destroyed as a result of battle. This extra effect does not apply to previous …"
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "including the "If the monster that this card is equipped to is destroyed in battle, this card is destroyed instead." effect."
  - does-not-address, Konami per-set Card Rulings documents, 2010-2011 (yugioh-card.com, Internet Archive) (2010-04-06): "1 Union Monster at a time. If the equipped monster would be destroyed, destroy this card instead.)"
- **d3** (supported): Era: the 1000 ATK loss ends when Armored Cybern is unequipped or leaves the field. Modern: the loss is permanent ('even if this card leaves the field').
  - V: Variant registers an EFFECT_UPDATE_ATTACK equip effect with RESET_EVENT+RESETS_STANDARD, so the ATK returns when Armored Cybern leaves; modern applies UpdateAttack(-1000) permanently. The variant implements the era way.
  - why: The UDE FAQ entry for Armored Cybern says the lost ATK is no longer applied once the card is unequipped or removed from the field. At Tengu: Same FAQ entry in force; nothing later replaces it.

- **d4** (contradicted) *(not a claim of the record: a point of the script or another event)*: Script-only point (not part of the erratum claim): when the equipped monster's ATK cannot be reduced (for example it is unaffected by Spell Cards) the era effect resolves with no effect and does not destroy the target. The modern script only destroys if UpdateAttack(-1000) returns -1000.
  - V: Variant checks only that the equipped monster's ATK is at least 1000, registers the -1000 effect, then destroys the target with no check that the reduction applied; on the diff alone it would still destroy the target for an equipped monster the ATK loss does not affect.   - why: The UDE FAQ says the effect can be activated but disappears when the ATK cannot be reduced; the variant script as read does not implement that (engine behaviour untested). At Tengu: Same FAQ entry in force at Tengu.
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "When "Armored Cybern" is equipped to a monster that is unaffected by the effects of Spell Cards, you can activate its effect but the effect disappears because the equipped monster's ATK cannot be reduced with "Armored Cybern's" effect."

Machina Mayhem and later PDFs print Unions as 'would be destroye…; UDE judge-list threads: Armored Cybern, Union → Nothing.
- variant-script points a ruling contradicts (listed for the owner, no edit): Armored Cybern: the variant script destroys the target even when the equipped monster's ATK cannot be reduced, where the card FAQ says the effect then disappears (read from the script, not run).

#### Axe of Despair (`axe-of-despair.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream, unresolved, AMBIG / reuse-upstream, unresolved, AMBIG. Action: **c2: cosmetic**.

  - V: Not in the script diff: archetype membership sits in the cdb setcode, and the record notes that the goat cdb entry already carries setcode 0x45 (Archfiend), i.e. the variant already treats Axe of Despair as an Archfiend.

Nothing on Archfien…; UDE judge-list threads (three Axe of Despair threads 2007): Axe of Despair, Archfiend, timing → Threads are about Limiter Removal, Megamorph and stacking ATK; nothing on Archfiend or tr…
- variant-script points a ruling contradicts (listed for the owner, no edit): Axe of Despair: the variant script (from the undated c0 ruling event, left alone) can return the card even after its trigger missed timing; the card FAQ (after Cyber Jar) says the timing has passed.

#### Big Shield Gardna (`big-shield-gardna.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: the negation is a mandatory continuous effect applied when the targeting Spell Card resolves, not an activated effect that goes on the chain, so it cannot be responded to. Modern: a Quick Effect (chain link) that triggers when the face-down card is targeted.
  - V: Variant registers the negation as a global EVENT_CHAIN_ACTIVATING continuous effect (upstream comment: 'a continuous effect rather than quick'), so it never goes on the chain; the modern script uses EFFECT_TYPE_QUICK_F on EVENT_CHAINING. The variant implements the era way on this point.
  - why: UDE ruling: Big Shield Gardna negates the targeting Spell when that Spell resolves, and the FAQ lets a Book of Moon chained to the Spell change its state before the negation applies. Both place the negation at resolution of the Spell, not as a chain link of its own. Neither says outright that it cannot be responded to… At Tengu: Same UDE ruling and FAQ entry still in force; no later Konami document changes the card (it is not in any errata list up to 2010-11-05).
  - supports, UDE Judge List, "big shield gardna vs. soul exchange" (Internet Archive; capture 2007-11-18) (2007-09-25): ""Big Shield Gardna" will negate "Soul Exchange" when "Soul Exchange" resolves."
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If "Big Shield Gardna" is targeted with a Spell Card, you may chain "Book of Moon" to flip it face-down, then it can negate the Spell Card and will flip back to face-up Defense Position."
- **d2** (unresolved): Era: 'negate that card's activation and change this card to face-up Defense Position' are unconditional and independent. Modern: the negation happens only if the change to face-up Defense Position succeeds ('and if you do').
  - V: Variant calls ChangePosition and NegateActivation one after the other with no check on the position change; modern negates only if Duel.ChangePosition succeeds.
  - why: No ruling covers a case where the position change cannot happen (for example Big Shield Gardna cannot change position); the Skill Drain line only says it 'will still be flipped'. At Tengu: Same as Edison.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "It will still be flipped to face-up Defense Position, and now its effect that changes its battle position when it is attacked will be negated."
- **d3** (contradicted) *(not a claim of the record: a point of the script or another event)*: Script-only point (not part of the erratum claim): the era negation is decided when the targeting Spell resolves, so a face-up Big Shield Gardna targeted by a Spell can be flipped face-down by a chained Book of Moon and then negate it. The variant script tests 'face-down and the sole target' only at EVENT_CHAIN_ACTIVATING and negates the activation right there.
  - V: negop runs on EVENT_CHAIN_ACTIVATING, requires tc:IsFacedown() at that moment, then calls ChangePosition and NegateActivation(ev). A face-up Big Shield Gardna that is flipped face-down afterwards (Book of Moon) would not negate the Spell. The script also negates the activation (not just the effect) at chain time, wher…
  - why: The UDE FAQ says a Book of Moon chained to the targeting Spell lets Big Shield Gardna negate it; the variant script, as read, cannot do that because it checks only at activation. At Tengu: Same FAQ entry still in force.
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If "Big Shield Gardna" is targeted with a Spell Card, you may chain "Book of Moon" to flip it face-down, then it can negate the Spell Card and will flip back to face-up Defense Position."
  - contradicts, UDE Judge List, "big shield gardna vs. soul exchange" (Internet Archive; capture 2007-11-18) (2007-09-25): ""Big Shield Gardna" will negate "Soul Exchange" when "Soul Exchange" resolves."

#### Brain Control (`brain-control.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: Brain Control targets any face-up monster the opponent controls, including Extra Deck monsters (Fusion, Synchro). Modern: only monsters 'that can be Normal Summoned/Set', so Extra Deck monsters are excluded.
  - V: Variant filter is IsControlerCanBeChanged() and IsFaceup() with no IsSummonableCard(); the modern script adds c:IsSummonableCard(). The variant implements the era way.
  - why: The Absolute Powerforce ruling (Feb 2010, before Edison) has the opponent using Brain Control to take control of a Synchro Monster (the one you took with Tuner's Scheme), so Brain Control could take Synchro Monsters. Indirect: it is a ruling on Tuner's Scheme, not a statement of Brain Control's target range. At Tengu: Same ruling in force (also in the v1.1 ABPF booklet, 2011-05); no Konami document up to Tengu, including the errata lists, restricts Brain Control's targets.

  - supports, Konami "ABSOLUTE POWERFORCE – CARD RULINGS Compiled as of May 12, 2011 - <version 1.1>" (Internet Archive capture) (2011-05-12): "If your opponent uses "Brain Control" to take control of the monster you took control of with "Tuner's Scheme" and the monster is destroyed, "Tuner's Scheme" is destroyed as well."
- **d2** (unresolved): Era: Brain Control may also take monsters that cannot be Normal Summoned or Set (for example Special Summon-only main Deck monsters such as Toon monsters). Modern: excluded.
  - V: Same missing IsSummonableCard() test as d1, so any face-up monster is a legal target.
  - why: No corpus ruling says whether Brain Control (or Change of Heart / Snatch Steal) could take a Special Summon-only monster from the main Deck; the Synchro ruling in d1 covers Extra Deck monsters only. At Tengu: Same as Edison.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "You cannot activate "Brain Control" if you already have 5 monsters on your side of the field."

#### Clear World (`clear-world.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

#### Cyber Blader (`cyber-blader.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: with exactly 3 opposing monsters, the card switches off the effects of the opponent's Spell, Trap and Effect Monster cards on the field (active effects as well as activations); modern: it only negates effects the opponent activates.

  - why: The same FAQ line says the effect will not negate Sangan or D.D. Survivor, whose effects activate from the Graveyard or banished zone. At Tengu: Same ruling; no later Konami document in the corpus changes it.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12): "When the opponent has exactly 3 monsters, "Cyber Blader"'s effect only negates the effects of Spell, Trap, and Effect Monster Cards that activate or are active on the field. #### Imperial Order (`imperial-order.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved): Era: the maintenance cost applies only during its controller's own Standby Phase ('During each of your Standby Phases'); modern: it applies in every Standby Phase, including the opponent's ('Once per turn, during the Standby Phase').
  - V: Adds SetCondition(s.mtcon) with mtcon returning Duel.GetTurnPlayer()==tp, so the maintenance trigger fires only in the controller's own Standby Phase. The modern script drops the condition.
  - why: Period entries that mention Imperial Order's payment place it in the controller's Standby Phase, but none says whether it is or is not also paid in the opponent's Standby Phase. At Tengu: Same corpus, nothing later addresses it; the record's existing call stands.

  - V: Gates the payment on Duel.SelectYesNo (the controller may decline and Imperial Order is destroyed). The modern script pays automatically whenever Duel.CheckLPCost(tp,700) succeeds, with no choice.
  - why: The UDE card FAQ (Konami-hosted, 2008-12) says the controller can choose not to pay the 700 Life Points and Imperial Order is then destroyed; the Ancient Prophecy ruling likewise treats 'not paying' as an ordinary outcome. At Tengu: Same rulings; no later Konami document in the corpus (errata lists, rulebooks, Tengu-only set PDFs) replaces or contradicts them.

#### Jirai Gumo (`jirai-gumo.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: the coin toss is a cost of declaring the attack (no chain link, cannot be responded to or negated as a chain effect); modern: it is a mandatory Trigger Effect on attack declaration that starts a chain and can be responded to.
  - V: Registers the toss as EFFECT_ATTACK_COST guarded by Duel.IsAttackCostPaid/Duel.AttackCostPaid, i.e. a cost with no chain link. The modern script registers a mandatory EFFECT_TYPE_TRIGGER_F on EVENT_ATTACK_ANNOUNCE with a target function and operation info (a chain link).
  - why: The UDE card FAQ (Konami-hosted, 2008-12) groups Jirai Gumo's toss with Dark Elf's payment as a 'cost' to attack; a cost is paid, not put on a chain, which is the era shape the variant script implements. At Tengu: Same FAQ line; no later Konami document in the corpus (errata lists, rulebooks, Tengu-only set PDFs) says anything different about this card.
  - supports, Konami TCG Skilled White Magician Card FAQ (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "However, all other costs are negated, so you do not have to pay to attack with "Dark Elf" or "Jirai Gumo"."

The two differ by 1 LP when the total is odd.
  - V: Sets the controller's Life Points to Duel.GetLP(tp)//2 (floor) on a wrong call; the modern script sets math.ceil(Duel.GetLP(tp)/2).
  - why: The UDE FAQ states that when halving your Life Points leaves a fraction, your Life Points are rounded up, which is the modern (ceil) result, so the variant script's floor is wrong on this point. #### King Tiger Wanghu (`king-tiger-wanghu.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: when King Tiger Wanghu is Summoned at the same time as other monsters with 1400 or less ATK (e.g. by one Cyber Jar resolution), its effect triggers and destroys those other monsters; modern: it does not activate at all when it is itself part of the summoned group ('must be face-up on the field to activate').
  - V: Its trigger condition omits the modern 'not eg:IsContains(e:GetHandler())' clause, so the trigger fires when Wanghu is in the summoned group and destroys the qualifying monsters in that group. The modern script suppresses the trigger in that case.

  - why: The FAQ entry says Wanghu destroys itself when Normal or Special Summoned with its own ATK reduced below 1400 by a pre-existing effect. At Tengu: Same FAQ entry; nothing later in the corpus addresses it.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ik page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If "King Tiger Wanghu" is Normal Summoned or Special Summoned, and its own ATK is reduced below 1400 by a pre-existing effect, then it destroys itself when Summoned."

- **d1** (supported): Era: the effect is a Continuous Effect that applies by itself when Makyura is sent to the Graveyard (no chain link, cannot be responded to, not optional); modern: an optional Trigger Effect that you activate and that starts a chain.
  - V: Registers EFFECT_TYPE_SINGLE+EFFECT_TYPE_CONTINUOUS on EVENT_TO_GRAVE with no condition: the Trap-from-hand permission is applied automatically. The modern script is EFFECT_TYPE_SINGLE+EFFECT_TYPE_TRIGGER_O with EFFECT_FLAG_DELAY.

  - V: No previous-location condition: applies for any EVENT_TO_GRAVE of the card. The modern script requires IsPreviousLocation(LOCATION_MZONE).

  - V: No SetCountLimit on the trigger; the modern script has SetCountLimit(1,id).
  - why: Nothing in the corpus addresses multiple copies or repeated use in one turn. At Tengu: Same.

#### Night Assailant (`night-assailant.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: known-gap / reuse-upstream. Action: **c0: checked, c1: checked**.

- **d1** (contradicted): Era (AST-080 wording, which names no exception): the Night Assailant that was just sent from the hand to the Graveyard is itself an eligible choice for its own return effect; the DT03-EN056 text and the modern text exclude it.
  - V: The pre-errata script excludes the physical card (SelectTarget exception e:GetHandler()), i.e. the 'except this card' behaviour. It therefore does not implement the self-eligible reading; if the claim were true the script would be wrong, and the rulings say the claim is false.
  - why: The UDE card ruling in force says the Night Assailant that itself went to the Graveyard cannot be selected, and gives the reason (no infinite loop). This is the same behaviour as the shipped script. At Tengu: Same UDE ruling still in force; no later Konami document in the corpus touches the card (not on any errata list).
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "You cannot select the "Night Assailant" that was itself sent to the Graveyard, so there is no infinite loop."
  - contradicts, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "You cannot select the "Night Assailant" that was itself sent to the Graveyard, so there is no infinite loop."
- **d2** (contradicted): Era (AST-080 'return 1 Flip Effect Monster from your Graveyard to your hand', no 'select'): the return effect does not target. DT03-EN056 and modern add an explicit selection.
  - V: The pre-errata script targets on both effects (EFFECT_FLAG_CARD_TARGET, Duel.SelectTarget in the return effect), same as the modern script.
  - why: UDE ruling: both of Night Assailant's effects target, so the return effect already selected a target under the AST-era wording. At Tengu: Same ruling still in force.

#### Nutrient Z (`nutrient-z.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: activation window is the opponent's Damage Step, at the 'Calculate and Apply Damage' portion or any point in the Damage Step before that. Modern: a plain trigger during damage calculation when you are about to take 2000+ battle damage.
  - V: The GOAT script registers a global EVENT_PRE_BATTLE_DAMAGE effect (fires when 2000+ battle damage is about to be dealt) that offers the activation, then activates an EVENT_FREE_CHAIN effect gated by a flag. It therefore offers the card at the Calculate-and-Apply-Damage moment only, not at the earlier Damage Step point…
  - why: UDE ruling: the card can be activated during the opponent's Damage Step at the Calculate-and-Apply-Damage portion or at any earlier point in the Damage Step. The script covers the Calculate-and-Apply moment (the case that matters for a 2000+ damage check) but not the earlier points. At Tengu: Same ruling in force; rulebooks v7.1/v7.2/v8 keep the general Damage Step limits (Counter Traps and ATK/DEF changers only, before damage calculation), which this card overrides for itself.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "This card can only be activated during your opponent's Damage Step during the "Calculate and Apply Damage" portion or at any point in the Damage Step prior to that."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "This card can only be activated during your opponent's Damage Step during the "Calculate and Apply Damage" portion or at any point in the Damage Step prior to that."
- **d2** (contradicted) *(not a claim of the record: a point of the script or another event)*: Variant script only: the activation window is also offered while the card is in the HAND (condition2 accepts LOCATION_HAND+LOCATION_SZONE), whereas the modern card is a Trap that is activated from the Spell/Trap Zone.
  - V: The global EVENT_PRE_BATTLE_DAMAGE effect returns true when the card is in the hand or Spell/Trap Zone and then calls Duel.Activate on its activation effect, so the script is written to let the card be activated from the hand. The modern script has no hand path.
  - why: Rulebook v7.0/v7.1 (both in force): 'Before you can activate a Trap Card, you must Set it on the field first.' A Trap in the hand cannot be activated, so a hand path is not a period behaviour. Caveat: whether ocgcore actually lets the script's hand path succeed was not tested (this audit reads the corpus only). At Tengu: Same rule, repeated in v7.2 and v8.
  - contradicts, Konami "Official Rulebook" Version 7.0 (archived 2008-12-30) (2008): "Before you can activate a Trap Card, you must Set it on the field first."
  - contradicts, Konami "OFFICIAL RULEBOOK Version 7.1" (archived 2010-03-30, 25 days before SJC Edison) (2010-03-30): "Before you can activate a Trap Card, you must Set it on the field first."
- **d3** (supported): Era: while Nutrient Z is on the chain in the Damage Step, only Counter Traps (and another Nutrient Z) can be chained to it: Kuriboh, Bad Reaction to Simochi and similar cannot. Modern: no such chain limit.
  - V: Duel.SetChainLimitTillChainEnd with s.chlimit: allows only Counter Trap activations and any card coded Nutrient Z. The modern script has no chain limit.
  - why: UDE rulings: Kuriboh cannot be activated in a chain to Nutrient Z, Bad Reaction to Simochi cannot be chained in the Damage Step, and two copies of Nutrient Z can both be activated (gain 8000). All three fit a Counter-Trap-plus-Nutrient-Z chain limit. At Tengu: Same rulings in force; rulebooks v7.1-v8 repeat that only Counter Traps and ATK/DEF-changing cards can be activated in the Damage Step.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "You cannot activate "Kuriboh" in a chain to "Nutrient Z"'s effect."
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "You can activate 2 copies of "Nutrient Z" and you will gain 8000 Life Points if you would take 2000 points or more of Battle Damage."
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If the opponent activates "Nutrient Z" in the Damage Step, you cannot chain "Bad Reaction to Simochi" since it cannot be activated in the Damage Step."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "You cannot activate "Kuriboh" in a chain to "Nutrient Z"'s effect."
  - supports, Konami "Official Rulebook" Version 7.0 (archived 2008-12-30) (2008): "During the Damage Step, you can only activate Counter Trap Cards, or cards with effects that directly change a monster's ATK or DEF."

#### Paladin of White Dragon (`paladin-of-white-dragon.json`)

- **d1** (unresolved): Era parenthetical '("Blue-Eyes White Dragon" cannot attack during that turn.)': only the monster just Summoned by this effect cannot attack. Modern (2013 erratum): every Blue-Eyes White Dragon on either field, including ones already there and ones Summoned later that turn, cannot attack for the rest of the turn.

The modern script registers a field effect (TargetRange MZONE,MZONE) matching every card coded Blue-Eyes White Dragon until End Phase. The variant …
  - why: No entry in the corpus rules on how far the parenthetical reaches for Paladin of White Dragon. The nearest text is a different card (Burst Stream of Destruction), whose ruling is name-wide; it does not say Paladin works the same way. At Tengu: Same: nothing new by Tengu (no Konami document in the corpus mentions Paladin of White Dragon except through Divine Wrath and Skill Drain).

#### Rescue Cat (`rescue-cat.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

#### Ring of Destruction (`ring-of-destruction.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved (general rule only)): Era: no turn condition; a Set Ring of Destruction can be activated during either player's turn. Modern (2015): only during your opponent's turn.
  - V: The pre-errata script has no condition function (no turn check); the modern one has s.condition = Duel.IsTurnPlayer(1-tp). The variant implements the any-turn reading.
  - why: General rule only: the rulebook says a Set Trap can be activated at any time from the turn after it is Set, and Ring of Destruction's own FAQ entry names no turn restriction (its one restriction is the Damage Step). No ruling addresses Ring on its controller's own turn specifically. At Tengu: Same rulebook rule in v7.1, v7.2 and v8; entry unchanged.
  - supports, Konami "Official Rulebook" Version 7.0 (archived 2008-12-30) (2008): "You cannot activate a Trap in the same turn that you Set it, but you can activate it at any time after that"
  - supports, Konami "OFFICIAL RULEBOOK Version 7.1" (archived 2010-03-30, 25 days before SJC Edison) (2010-03-30): "You cannot activate a Trap in the same turn that you Set it, but you can activate it at any time after that"
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_pr page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "You cannot activate "Ring of Destruction" during the Damage Step."
- **d2** (unresolved): Era: may target any face-up monster on the field, either player's. Modern: only a face-up monster your opponent controls.
  - V: The pre-errata script's target filter is any face-up destructible monster on both fields (LOCATION_MZONE,LOCATION_MZONE); the modern one is 0,LOCATION_MZONE with an ATK ceiling.
  - why: Every period ruling that uses Ring has the opponent chaining it to destroy the other player's monster; none says it can or cannot target your own monster. At Tengu: Same.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_pr page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "This card targets a monster."
- **d3** (unresolved): Era: no ceiling on the target's ATK. Modern: the target's ATK must be at most your opponent's Life Points.
  - V: The pre-errata filter is IsFaceup and IsDestructable only; the modern filter adds IsAttackBelow(opponent's LP).
  - why: No ruling covers a Ring whose target has more ATK than someone's Life Points. The Witch of the Black Forest entry has Ring reducing a player to zero, which fits the era reading but does not test the case above their LP. At Tengu: Same.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_pr page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "If this reduces the player's Life Points to zero, then they cannot use "Witch of the Black Forest"'s effect (to retrieve a 5th Exodia piece, for example)."
- **d4** (supported): Era: the damage equals the destroyed monster's CURRENT ATK (counters, Equips, doublers apply). Modern: equal to its ORIGINAL ATK.
  - V: The pre-errata script reads tc:GetAttack() (current ATK) before destroying; the modern script reads tc:GetTextAttack() (original). The variant implements the era way.
  - why: The UDE entry says Ring uses the current (total) ATK, not the original, and a UDE Netrep answer applies a doubled ATK (Limiter Removal) as the damage. At Tengu: Same ruling and answer in force; nothing later replaces them.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_pr page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): ""Ring of Destruction" inflicts damage based on the current (total) ATK value, NOT the original ATK."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): ""Ring of Destruction" inflicts damage based on the current (total) ATK value, NOT the original ATK."
  - supports, UDE Judge List, "Ring of Destruction vs Limiter Removal" (Internet Archive; capture 2007-11-01) (2007-08-12): ""Limiter Removal" resolves first and doubles "Cyber Dragon's" ATK, from 2100 to 4200."
- **d5** (supported): Era: Ring deals the full damage to BOTH players at once. Modern: you take the damage first, then your opponent takes an equal amount (and nothing if you are already at 0 Life Points).
  - V: The pre-errata script calls Duel.Damage on both players with the 'no result' flag and closes with Duel.RDComplete(), i.e. one simultaneous damage event; the modern script damages you, then BreakEffect, then your opponent.
  - why: UDE answers and FAQ lines state that Ring inflicts the same damage to both players ('to both players' Life Points', 'to each player', 'both players'). None spells out the word 'simultaneous', but all treat it as one two-sided damage rather than a you-then-them sequence. At Tengu: Same answers in force.
  - supports, UDE Judge List, "Ring of Destruction vs Limiter Removal" (Internet Archive; capture 2007-11-01) (2007-08-12): ""Ring of Destruction" resolves next and destroys the "Cyber Dragon", inflicting 4200 damage to both players' Life Points."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "If I chain "Ring of Destruction" to the placement of a Spell Counter on "Breaker, the Magical Warrior", "Ring of Destruction" does 1600 damage to each player because the action of placing the Spell Counter has not resolved."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "If a monster equipped with "Heavy Mech Support Platform" is targeted by "Ring of Destruction", then "Heavy Mech Support Platform" is destroyed instead of the equipped monster, and the damage inflicted to both players by "Ring of Destruction" is 0"
- **d6** (unresolved): Era: no limit on Rings per turn. Modern: 'You can only activate 1 "Ring of Destruction" per turn'.
  - V: The pre-errata script has no SetCountLimit; the modern one has SetCountLimit(1,id,EFFECT_COUNT_CODE_OATH).
  - why: No ruling activates or forbids two copies in a turn. At Tengu: Same.

#### Ryko, Lightsworn Hunter (`ryko-lightsworn-hunter.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era: the destruction targets (the card is chosen at activation). Modern (2017): no targeting; the card is chosen at resolution.
  - V: The pre-errata script sets EFFECT_FLAG_CARD_TARGET and picks the card in the target function (Duel.SelectTarget after a yes/no), then destroys Duel.GetFirstTarget(); the modern script has no target and chooses at resolution. The variant implements the targeting reading.
  - why: The UDE FAQ entry says that if you choose to destroy a card, the effect targets that card. At Tengu: Same entry in force; nothing later replaces it.

#### Senet Switch (`senet-switch.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: cosmetic**.

- **d1** (contradicted): Era Senet Switch designates the destination Monster Card Zone when the effect is activated and does nothing if that zone is filled before resolution; the pre-errata script instead picks any still-free adjacent zone at resolution (the modern script stores the zone at activation and aborts if it is filled).

#### Sinister Serpent (`sinister-serpent.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era Sinister Serpent has no once-per-turn limit; the modern card can use its effect only once per turn (hard limit by name).
  - V: The Standby Phase TRIGGER_O has no SetCountLimit, so it can activate again the same turn (for example after a negated activation, or for a second copy). The modern script adds e1:SetCountLimit(1,id).
  - why: The UDE card FAQ says that after Divine Wrath negates Sinister Serpent's effect the opponent 'can activate the effect of Sinister Serpent again ... even during the same Standby Phase', which a once-per-turn effect (where the negated activation still counts) would forbid.   - V: Operation only does Duel.SendtoHand; it registers no End Phase banish effect. The modern script registers a one-shot EVENT_PHASE+PHASE_END effect (opponent's turn) that banishes a copy.
  - why: No entry in the corpus mentions any banish or end-of-turn cost for Sinister Serpent; the era entry lists only optionality, activation from any Graveyard route and negation interactions. Absence is not proof. At Tengu: Same; nothing later addresses it. The record's existing call stands.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If you discard "Sinister Serpent" during your Standby Phase for the effect of "Mirage of Nightmare", you may add "Sinister Serpent" back to your hand during that same Standby Phase."

Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

  - why: No ruling on this card. By analogy, Konami's Raging Battle ruling and the UDE Cybernetic Magician ruling say modifiers applied after an 'ATK becomes' effect adjust from the new value, which the final-set script would not do; but those rulings concern one-shot effects, so no contradiction of the script is asserted. #### Super Rejuvenation (`super-rejuvenation.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved): Era Super Rejuvenation credits a Dragon Tributed from the field to its previous controller (a Dragon of yours that your opponent Tributes still counts for you); the modern card credits it to the player who performed the Tribute.
  - V: The tracking loop takes p=tc:GetPreviousControler() for both field and hand cases, so a Tribute of your Dragon by the opponent counts for you. The modern script uses tc:GetReasonPlayer() for a field Tribute (previous controller for hand discards).
  - why: The card's UDE entry addresses only DNA Surgery, timing, cumulative copies and Solemn Wishes; it never says whose Tribute counts. Other period rulings on opponents Tributing your monsters (Soul Exchange, Zolga) concern other cards' own effects. At Tengu: Same corpus. The Tengu-only Storm of Ragnarok Shinai ruling ('the original owner ... activates the effect, regardless of who actually Tributes Shinai') is about Shinai's own trigger effect, not counting for Super Rejuvenation.

Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

  - why: The period FAQ says the chosen Ultimate Tyranno attacks all the opponent's monsters 'first', which is the era ordering rule; nothing in it says your other monsters may not attack at all. At Tengu: Same ruling in force; no later Konami document in the corpus replaces it.

#### W-Wing Catapult (`w-wing-catapult.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era text carries the Union condition '(A monster can only be equipped with 1 Union Monster at a time)': a V-Tiger Jet already carrying a Union monster cannot be equipped with this card. The modern card has no such limit.
  - V: The pre-errata script calls aux.AddUnionProcedure(c,s.filter,true) with s.filter requiring c:GetUnionCount()==0, so a target already carrying a Union monster is not a legal equip target; the modern script uses the plain code filter with no Union count check.
  - why: Period rulings apply the one-Union-per-monster limit to Union Monsters as a condition (Cyber Phoenix / Heavy Mech Support Platform entry) and have surplus Union Monsters destroyed when several are moved onto one target (Collected Power). They are about Union Monsters in general and do not name W-Wing Catapult. At Tengu: Same rulings in force; nothing in the corpus replaces them, and the Tengu-only Konami documents only print the same limit on new Union cards.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "(The Machine-Type monster can still only be equipped with 1 Union Monster at a time, because that is a condition, not an effect)."
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If multiple Union Monsters are going to be equipped to 1 legal target monster by "Collected Power", you select 1 of them and equip it to the target monster, and the other Union Monster Cards are destroyed."
- **d2** (supported): Era destruction replacement is battle-only ('If the equipped monster would be destroyed by battle, destroy this card instead'); the modern text also replaces destruction by card effect.
  - V: Auxiliary.UnionReplace(true), used through the old-union flag, returns true only for REASON_BATTLE; the modern script's replacement also covers destruction by card effect.
  - why: The period FAQ says a Union Monster with a replacement for destruction in general is different from 'previous Union Monsters', which only replace destruction in battle. At Tengu: Same ruling in force; the per-set documents from Machina Mayhem on show new Union cards with the wider wording, which the Heavy Mech Support Platform ruling already separates from older Union Monsters.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "Note that "Heavy Mech Support Platform" is different from other Union Monsters in that its last sentence is applied if the monster it is equipped to is destroyed (period), not just when the monster is destroyed as a result of battle. This extra effect does not apply to previous …"
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "including the "If the monster that this card is equipped to is destroyed in battle, this card is destroyed instead." effect."
- **d3** (supported): Era: 'When equipped to a monster by this card's effect, the equipped monster gains 400 ATK and DEF', and the unequip option only works in proper Union state; modern: a monster equipped with this card gains 400 ATK/DEF however it was equipped.
  - V: The pre-errata script puts e:SetCondition(aux.IsUnionState) on the 400 ATK and DEF effects and on the old-union unequip option, so the bonus only applies when the card was equipped by its own effect; the modern script's equip effects have no such condition.
  - why: The period FAQ says a Union Monster equipped by some other card (Union Rider) does not give its bonuses, while one equipped with Formation Union counts as equipped by its effect and does. At Tengu: Same rulings in force; no later Konami document in the corpus replaces them.

  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "The Union Monster is considered to be equipped 'by its effect' so any bonuses the Union Monster grants are granted if equipped with "Formation Union"."
- **d4** (unresolved): Era unequip Special Summons the card in face-up Attack Position; the modern text just says 'Special Summon it' (a position of the player's choice).
  - V: The old-union procedure Special Summons with POS_FACEUP_ATTACK; the modern procedure lets the controller choose the position.
  - why: No ruling in the corpus addresses the position a Union Monster returns in when it unequips; the Combination Attack entry only assumes a Union Monster came back in face-up Attack Position. At Tengu: Same; the Konami per-set documents only reprint the card text 'in face-up Attack Position' for other Union cards.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "Both the equipped monster and the Union Monster can attack after "Combination Attack" is used, assuming the Union Monster was Special Summoned in face-up Attack Position."

#### Witch of the Black Forest (`witch-of-the-black-forest.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream, AMBIG. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved): Era effect has no name-lock: the card added to hand (and copies of it) can still be activated or used the same turn. The 2017 text bars activating cards or effects with that name for the rest of the turn.
  - why: The card's own FAQ entry says nothing about restrictions after the search and no ruling in the corpus discusses using the added card in the same turn. At Tengu: Same; none of the Tengu-only documents addresses it.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "You must show your opponent the monster you select from your Deck for this card's effect."
- **d2** (unresolved): Era effect can resolve more than once per turn (no limit, each copy triggers on its own); the 2017 text says the effect of 'Witch of the Black Forest' can be used only once per turn.
  - why: No ruling in the corpus covers two copies of Witch of the Black Forest being sent to the Graveyard in one turn; the rulings on the card treat one Witch at a time. At Tengu: Same.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_lo page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "If you Tribute "Sangan" and "Witch of the Black Forest" to Tribute Summon "Light and Darkness Dragon", the effect of "Sangan" and "Witch of the Black Forest" form a Chain."

  - why: The period FAQ says that with no eligible monster the opponent gets to see the Deck to verify, and a Konami per-set document (Machina Mayhem, 2010-04-06) applies the same verification to a comparable Deck search. At Tengu: The FAQ ruling is still in force and no later Konami document in the corpus replaces it, but the last Konami confirmation of the practice is Machina Mayhem in April 2010 (the record cites 2011-02-02 for other cards).
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "If you have no eligible monsters, your opponent gets to see your Deck to verify."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment per-card FAQ, cards U-Z (Internet Archive capture 2009-02-26; the other letter pages were captured 2009-02-17 to 2009-02-26) (2009-02-26): "If you have no eligible monsters, your opponent gets to see your Deck to verify."

  - supports, Konami per-set Card Rulings documents, 2010-2011 (yugioh-card.com, Internet Archive) (2010-04-06): "If you do not have "Commander Covington" in your Deck, your opponent can ask to verify."

#### Wulf, Lightsworn Beast (`wulf-lightsworn-beast.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

#### XY-Dragon Cannon (`xy-dragon-cannon.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream, unresolved, AMBIG / reuse-upstream, unresolved, AMBIG. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved (inference only)): Era XY-Dragon Cannon cannot be Special Summoned by any route except its own contact procedure (strict nomi, so it cannot come back from banishment after a proper summon); the modern card is a semi-nomi that can be Special Summoned from banishment once properly summoned, and only the Graveyard is barred.
  - V: Passes true as the summon-condition to Fusion.AddContactProc, which registers an EFFECT_SPSUMMON_CONDITION with no value: every Special Summon of the card other than its own contact procedure is forbidden, including from the banished zone. The modern script passes s.splimit (bars only Extra Deck and Graveyard sources)…
  - why: The FAQ in force (Konami 2008-12 copy) bars only the Extra Deck route and the Graveyard, matching the modern restrictions, and it replaced the 2005 any-route wording; Dimension Distortion rulings let a properly summoned Special Summon-only monster return from banishment. At Tengu: Same as Edison; no later Konami document in the corpus changes the point (errata lists, rulebooks v7.2/v8 and per-set PDFs are silent on these cards).
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "These monsters cannot be Special Summoned from the Extra Deck except by the method described in their text."
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "They cannot be Special Summoned from the Graveyard even after they have been Special Summoned by the method described in their texts."
  - contradicts, Upper Deck Entertainment per-card FAQ, cards U-Z (Internet Archive capture 2009-02-26; the other letter pages were captured 2009-02-17 to 2009-02-26) (2009-02-26): "They cannot be Special Summoned from the Graveyard even after they have been Special Summoned by the method described in their texts."
  - supports, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "These monsters cannot be Special Summoned EXCEPT by the way described in their text."
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "Note that "Horus the Black Flame Dragon LV8" has the word "except" in its Summoning conditions, which means that even if Special Summoned properly, you cannot revive it with "Monster Reborn" afterwards if it is destroyed."
  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_de page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "When a "Special Summon-only" monster is removed, without returning to the hand or Deck, after it is Special Summoned properly, it can be Special Summoned by "Dimension Distortion"."
- **d2** (unresolved) *(not a claim of the record: a point of the script or another event)*: The era contact fusion could only use monsters in the Monster Zone as material; the modern card can also banish material from the Spell and Trap Zone (an equipped Union monster). This is NOT part of the c1 erratum claim: it belongs to the separate undated c0 ruling event.
  - V: Material filter gathers Card.IsAbleToRemoveAsCost from LOCATION_MZONE only (modern: LOCATION_ONFIELD), so a Y-Dragon Head equipped to X-Head Cannon cannot be used until it is unequipped. Implements the c0 ruling event, not c1.
  - why: No ruling says whether an equipped Union monster (an Equip Card in the Spell and Trap Zone) may be banished as contact-fusion material; the only text says the specified monsters must be removed. At Tengu: Same; nothing in the Tengu-only documents addresses it.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "You must remove from play the monsters specified; you cannot use "Goddess with the Third Eye", etc., because you are not performing a Fusion Summon."
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_uz page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "You CAN remove a face-down "X-Head Cannon", etc., from play to Special Summon one of these monsters from the Extra Deck."
- searched: card FAQ (konami-card-faq-2008-12-15/16/17 pages, ude-card-faq-2009-02-26-uz): own entry and every mention of the card and its material names (X-Head Cannon, Y-Dragon Head), the XYZ FUSION… → No own entry. The shared XYZ FUSION MONSTERS section (2008-12 Konami copy and 2009-02 UDE…; 2005 UDE card rulings page (ude-card-rulings-archive): same section and same cards → Older wording of the XYZ FUSION MONSTERS section: cannot be Special Summoned EXCEPT by th…; Konami errata lists 2009-07-30, 2010-01-05, 2010-11-05: the card names, XYZ, Dragon Cannon, Tank Cannon, Head Cannon → nothing; Konami rulebooks v7.0, v7.1, v7.2, v8: properly Special Summoned, card-effect Special Summon from hand/Deck/Graveyard, Fusion Deck, Union, removed f… → Only the generic rule that a card effect cannot Special Summon a monster from the hand, D…; per-set ruling PDFs (CRMS, RGBT, ANPR, SOVR, ABPF, HA/SDWS/ST09, Machina Mayhem, Shining Darkness, Extreme Victory, DREV, Storm of Ragnarok, Starstrike Blast, HA03, Gold Series 3): the card names; contact-fusion wording ("removing from play the above cards"); Special Summon from removed fr… → Only ANPR Armityle the Chaos Phantom (same printed contact-fusion wording, ruling about c…; UDE judge-list threads (ude-thread:*, ude-judge-list-*): the card names and Special Summon-only / removed-from-play questions → nothing on these cards

Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era Y-Dragon Head can only be equipped to a monster that carries no Union monster at all (printed Condition); the modern card only refuses a monster already carrying a Union of the same kind.
  - V: aux.AddUnionProcedure(...,true): UnionFilter uses ct1==0 (any Union already equipped blocks the target) instead of the modern ct2==0.

  - supports, Konami "THE SHINING DARKNESS SNEAK PEEK - CARD RULINGS, Compiled as of April 30, 2010 <version 1.0>" (Internet Archive capture 2010-06-01) (2010-04-30): "You cannot activate the effect to equip a Union monster if Delta Tri is already equipped with a Union monster."
- **d2** (supported): Era Y-Dragon Head replaces destruction of the equipped monster only when destroyed by battle; the modern card also replaces destruction by card effect.
  - V: UnionReplace old branch: (r&REASON_BATTLE)~=0 only (modern also accepts REASON_EFFECT).
  - why: The FAQ says the extra all-destruction protection is Heavy Mech Support Platform only and does not apply to earlier Union Monsters. At Tengu: Same ruling; nothing later in the corpus changes it.
  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "Note that "Heavy Mech Support Platform" is different from other Union Monsters in that its last sentence is applied if the monster it is equipped to is destroyed (period), not just when the monster is destroyed as a result of battle. This extra effect does not apply to previous …"
- **d3** (supported): Era Y-Dragon Head grants its 400 ATK/DEF boost and its destroy-instead protection only while it was equipped by its own effect; the modern card grants them however it was equipped.
  - V: e1 (the ATK/DEF boost) gets SetCondition(aux.IsUnionState), and with oldequip=true the destroy-substitute effect is also gated on IsUnionState; the modern script has neither gate on the boost and gates the substitute on any equip target.
  - why: The Union Rider ruling says a Union monster not equipped by its own effect gets no bonuses and no destroy-instead effect; Formation Union is written as a special case that counts. At Tengu: Same rulings; the Tengu-only corpus does not change the point.

  - supports, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_fh page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-15) (2008-12-15): "The Union Monster is considered to be equipped 'by its effect' so any bonuses the Union Monster grants are granted if equipped with "Formation Union"."

  - V: UnionSumTarget/UnionSumOperation with oldrule pass POS_FACEUP_ATTACK to IsCanBeSpecialSummoned and SpecialSummon (modern: POS_FACEUP).

  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "Both the equipped monster and the Union Monster can attack after "Combination Attack" is used, assuming the Union Monster was Special Summoned in face-up Attack Position."
- **d5** (unresolved) *(not a claim of the record: a point of the script or another event)*: Era Y-Dragon Head can use its unequip effect only while equipped by its own effect (the script gates it on union state); the modern card may unequip whenever it has an equip target.
  - V: With oldequip=true the unequip effect e2 has SetCondition(Auxiliary.IsUnionState) instead of GetEquipTarget(). Not in the printed text (the unequip clause carries no by-this-effect wording); the difference exists only in the script.
  - why: No ruling says whether a Union monster equipped by another card (Union Rider, Formation Union) may use its own unequip effect. At Tengu: Same.

#### A Hero Emerges (`a-hero-emerges.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: known-gap / known-gap. Action: **event: cosmetic**.

- **d1** (contradicted): Under the pre-2013 text a randomly selected monster that cannot be Special Summoned (Special Summon-only, Spirit) stays in the hand; the modern card sends it to the Graveyard.

  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "you can Special Summon her, but if you don't have it, then she is sent to the Graveyard."
  - contradicts, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "But if your opponent selects a Special Summon-only monster like "Dark Necrofear", or a Spirit Monster, it is sent to the Graveyard."

#### Blaze Accelerator (`blaze-accelerator.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: known-gap / known-gap. Action: **event: narrowed**.

- **d1** (contradicted): The era card pays the hand-send of the Pyro-Type monster as a cost at activation (before any response); the modern card performs the send on resolution.

  - contradicts, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "Sending 1 Pyro-Type monster with 500 or less ATK to the Graveyard is part of this card's effect. It is not a cost."

#### Tri-Blaze Accelerator (`tri-blaze-accelerator.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: known-gap / known-gap. Action: **event: narrowed**.

- **d1** (contradicted): Era (record's reading): the Pyro monster is sent from the hand as a cost paid on activation of the effect; modern: the send happens during resolution.

#### Curse of Royal (`curse-of-royal.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: modern, reuse-upstream, AMBIG / modern, reuse-upstream, AMBIG. Action: **event: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): Era card negates any Spell/Trap activation that would destroy exactly 1 Spell/Trap, whether or not that activation targets; the modern script only negates activations that carry the targeting flag.
  - V: The goat script removes the re:IsHasProperty(EFFECT_FLAG_CARD_TARGET) test; its condition is just EFFECT_TYPE_ACTIVATE, chain negatable, and a CATEGORY_DESTROY operation info of 1 Spell/Trap on the field. That is the era reading, and it matches the FAQ examples.
  - why: The UDE FAQ entry lists Solemn Judgment and Seven Tools of the Bandit among the cards Curse of Royal can be used against, and another FAQ entry says Solemn Judgment does not target the card it destroys. At Tengu: Same rulings in force; no later Konami document in the corpus mentions Curse of Royal.

- **d2** (unresolved): Modern requires that the activated card's chain info holds exactly one target card (its text: 'targets 1 Spell/Trap on the field (and no other cards)'); the goat script drops that count test but keeps a count of exactly one destroyed Spell/Trap on the field.
  - V: The goat script drops the '#g~=1' test on CHAININFO_TARGET_CARDS but keeps 'tc==1' on the CATEGORY_DESTROY operation info with all destroyed cards being Spell/Trap on the field. That is the FAQ's '1 (and ONLY 1) Spell or Trap Card' rule.
  - why: The FAQ fixes the count of destroyed Spell/Traps at exactly one; nothing says whether a card that also targets some other, non-Spell/Trap card could be negated. At Tengu: Same.
  - does-not-address, Konami TCG Card FAQ default_ac page (Internet Archive capture served 2008-12-16) (2008-12-16): "You can only activate "Curse of Royal" as a chain to a Spell or Trap Card that would destroy 1 (and ONLY 1) Spell or Trap Card."

#### Necrovalley (`necrovalley.json`)
Coverage at Edison / Tengu: known-gap / known-gap. Action: **c1: checked, c2: checked, c3: checked**.

- **d1** (unresolved (inference only)): Edison era (TU02 text, 'target'): an effect that moves a card out of a Graveyard (other than the effect's own card) without targeting it is not negated (the record's examples: Rekindling, Red-Eyes Darkness Metal Dragon); from the 2010-10 text on (and in c2, c3 and the modern card) it is negated.
  - V: The pre-errata script (which implements the 2012 c2 text) negates such effects too; no upstream script implements the Edison targeting-only text, so this point is a known gap at both snapshots.

Coverage at Edison / Tengu: reuse-upstream / reuse-upstream. Action: **c2: checked, c3: checked**.

- **d1** (supported): c2 (2012): monsters that cannot be turned face-down (Monster Tokens, which are always face-up) are left alone; in the era they are simply changed to Defense Position. Modern script flips only monsters that can be turned face-down.

Only c2 is a difference between the era and a later version, c3 undoes it.
  - V: The goat script recomputes the group at resolution with no SetTargetCard, so monsters that appeared after activation are flipped. The pre-errata script (c2 text) adds Duel.SetTargetCard(g) at activation and flips only the monsters still related to the effect.

  - does-not-address, Upper Deck Entertainment official Yu-Gi-Oh! Card Rulings page (Internet Archive replay) (2005-07): "The effect of this card is applied even when a Spirit Monster is returned to its owner's hand by the effect of another card, such as "Penguin Soldier"."
- searched: card FAQ (Konami-hosted UDE FAQ, 2008-12): Spirit's Invitation own entry; who selects; Spirit Monster returns to hand; Penguin Soldier; Earthbound Spiri… → Own entry: may decline the 500 Life Point payment; applies when another card returns the …; 2005 UDE card rulings page: Spirit's Invitation → Same two lines.; errata lists 2009-07-30, 2010-01-05, 2010-11-05: Spirit's Invitation → nothing; rulebooks v7.0, v7.1, v7.2, v8: definition of 'select'/'target'; Spirit Monsters → Glossary 'Effects that Target' (quoted); nothing card-specific.; per-set ruling PDFs: Spirit's Invitation; Spirit returns to hand → nothing; UDE threads: Spirit's Invitation → nothing
