## 7. Round 036's leftover questions (part D)

- **VW-Tiger Catapult, did the era discard effect target?** No ruling names the card. Two rulings on other cards bear on it and are recorded as not counted: the card FAQ says the first effect of the sibling "VWXYZ-Dragon Catapult Cannon" targets, and Konami's Raging Battle ruling says the Trigger Effect of "Blackwing - Elphin the Raven", worded "change the Battle Position of 1 monster your opponent controls", targets. They point to the era effect having targeted, which is what the 2018 wording says, so the cosmetic call stands; verdict `unresolved` at both snapshots. Recorded on the record's transition (`rulings_check`) and its notes. If a ruling says it did not target, the difference becomes a functional transition and a known gap.
- **D.D. Survivor returning during the next turn's End Phase.** The card FAQ (D-E, 2008-12) rules: "If "D.D. Survivor's" effect is activated in the End Phase, and it is removed from play again (...), its effect will activate during the next turn's End Phase." The errata'd text returns only a card banished this turn and once per turn, and Ignis's modern script (a flag reset each End Phase) does not return it on a later turn: the ruling shows play differed. **Recorded as a second functional transition of the same event** (`axis: end-phase-return-next-turn`, supported at Edison and at Tengu by a UDE-era ruling counted under the owner's decision). The same entry's "can only be activated once per turn" contradicts the record's original claim (no once-per-turn cap), so that transition is now cosmetic. The record stays a known gap.
- **Dark Necrofear: Tailor of the Fickle / Collected Power, and a negated Summon.** Both are card FAQ rulings on the era card (Konami-hosted 2008-12-15, D-E), supported at both snapshots. **Neither is shown to differ from the modern card**: nothing in the corpus states how the modern card plays on either, so they are added to the record's notes as evidence and no transition is added. A third ruling, found while reading its entries, bears on the c2 transition: "If "Chaos Command Magician" is targeted by "Dark Necrofear", that effect selects 1 target so the effect of "Dark Necrofear" is negated and it is not equipped to "Chaos Command Magician"." (card FAQ, 2008-12-16, A-C). That says the era End Phase equip targeted, which is the opposite of the c2 summary's "no target". The record is outside the audited set, so c2 was left as recorded and this is on the owner's list. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): corrected, see section 11.**
- **A second Swap Frog copy.** The Stardust Overdrive ruling (Konami, compiled 2009-10-29): "If you activate the effect of more than 1 "Swap Frog," you can only Normal Summon 1 time in addition to your regular Normal Summon or Set." That is what the modern text's "(You can only gain this effect once per turn.)" writes down, so **no difference is shown** and no transition is added; the ruling is recorded on the record's check as `does-not-address`, with the reason.
- **Machina Peacekeeper and Machina Gearframe, "unequip ... in face-up Attack Position".** Searched across the whole corpus: every Union entry of the card FAQ and the 2005 page, and the per-set documents that reproduce the clause as printed text (The Shining Darkness, Absolute Powerforce, Extreme Victory, Machina Mayhem). The only line that mentions the position is a "Combination Attack" entry that assumes it ("assuming the Union Monster was Special Summoned in face-up Attack Position"), and the Doitsu entry says its unequip effect is "applied normally" without a position. **No ruling in force supports the clause**, so the two generated scripts are not changed; the search is recorded on both cards' `rulings_check` and `not_reproduced`.

## 8. Thin evidence and conflicts, for the owner

**Correction, round 038 (2026-09-29).** This paragraph first said "No data was changed for anything in this list." That was wrong, and it is replaced by what follows. Round 037 did change data for ten entries of this list: (1) **Imperial Custom** and **Senet Switch**: a period ruling contradicted every difference the transition claimed, so the transition became cosmetic, the record cosmetic-only and complete on the modern implementation, and the Edison and Tengu list line for each moved from Project Ignis's pre-errata variant back to the modern card (the only two list changes of the round); (2) **Soul Rope**: its second transition (`c1`) became cosmetic and the record cosmetic-only on the modern implementation (no list line moved: it was a known gap, so the lists already used the modern card); (3) **Toon Summoned Skull**: its 2013 transition (`c2`) became cosmetic (no list change); (4) **Blaze Accelerator, Boss Rush, Red-Eyes Wyvern, Tri-Blaze Accelerator, Wild Fire** and **Anteatereatingant**: the summary was narrowed to what no ruling contradicts, the withdrawn claims were recorded in the summary and the review notes, the gap statement gained a dated withdrawal, and the transition stayed functional (no list change). Round 037 also changed data for records that are not on this list: Ancient Fairy Dragon (summary narrowed), Blackwing - Sirocco the Dawn (`c0` narrowed, `c2` cosmetic), and the records in section 6 made cosmetic or given a second transition; section 6 is the complete table (19 records). For every other entry below, round 037 added a `rulings_check`, dated review notes and sources, and changed no kind, summary or coverage. Three of the ten changes rest on thin evidence, and the owner should decide them as a product matter rather than take them because the suite is green: Imperial Custom (one Konami sentence settles maintenance-cost destruction only; other non-battle, non-effect destruction is not addressed), Soul Rope's second transition (the rulebook's definition of "destroyed" plus the absence of a card-specific Damage Step permission: an inference, of the kind section 3 demotes to `unresolved (general rule only)` elsewhere) and Senet Switch's second difference (the rulebook shows five Monster Zones). Each is what the card would do either way and what each answer rests on.

- Armored Cybern: whether the era card's destroy-instead effect covered only battle rests on a FAQ line about older Union monsters; no ruling names this 2010 Union.
- Blue-Eyes Toon Dragon: no ruling on whether a Flip Summon after Book of Moon re-imposes the attack lock, or on Level-scaled Tributes; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Brain Control: that the era card could take Extra Deck monsters rests on a ruling about Tuner's Scheme, not one on Brain Control; nothing addresses main-Deck Special Summon-only monsters.
- Brionac: no ruling on either point (repeatable use; bouncing your own cards); the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Burning Land: no ruling on the standing destruction of Field Spells or on activating with none on the field; the variant is kept on the printed era text and the upstream author's comment.
- Catapult Turtle: repeatable use rests on the general rule for Ignition Effects and the printed text; no ruling names the card on this point.
- Chaos Emperor Dragon - Envoy of the End: no ruling on its 'cannot activate other cards' rider; repeatable use rests on the general rule for Ignition Effects.
- Crush Card Virus: no ruling names the card; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Dark Strike Fighter: repeatable use and use in Main Phase 2 rest on general rules and the printed text; no ruling names the card on either.
- Destiny HERO - Disk Commander: no corpus document names the card; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Gilasaurus: no ruling says whether the era effect targeted; the record dates the targeting wording to a 2012 printing, after both snapshots, so the variant may ship a post-snapshot design.
- Imperial Custom: one Konami sentence settles maintenance-cost destruction only; other non-battle, non-effect destruction is not addressed. The list now uses the modern card.
- Imperial Order: whether the 700 Life Point payment was due only in its controller's own Standby Phase rests on the printed text alone.
- Makyura the Destructor: whether several Traps could be played from the hand in one turn and whether several Makyuras each grant it rest on the printed text alone.
- Manga Ryu-Ran: no ruling; the variant script's Level thresholds differ between its condition and its Tribute selection (a Level 5 copy can be summoned for free).
- Mysterious Puppeteer: no ruling on the Life Point gain from its own Summon; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Night Assailant (held back, not shipped): both period sources rule that BOTH of its effects target (UDE card rulings page and its 2008-12 Konami-hosted copy). Whether the effect was optional or mandatory: no ruling in words; the timing rulings (it activates as a cost or mid-chain, where the FAQ says optional 'when...you can' effects miss timing) point to mandatory. The AST-080 'self-eligible' claim is contradicted by the same entry, so the c0 change looks cosmetic and Ignis's pre-errata script already matches both snapshots on it.
- Paladin of White Dragon: no ruling on the attack ban's scope; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Red-Eyes Darkness Metal Dragon: no corpus document names the card; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Rescue Cat: no ruling on either point; the variant is kept on the printed era text.
- Ring of Destruction: use on its controller's own turn, targeting your own monster, no Life Point ceiling and no once-per-turn limit rest on the printed text alone.
- Sangan: the variant mapped to Edison and Tengu is the c0 pre-errata script, which has no Deck-verification step; the card FAQ and Konami's Machina Mayhem and Storm of Ragnarok rulings keep it in force. No ruling on a name lock or a once-per-turn limit.
- Senet Switch: the second difference (Main Monster Zone only) is not a ruling; the rulebook shows five Monster Zones, so the two texts play alike. The list now uses the modern card.
- Spirit Ryu: no corpus document names the card; whether its boost was Spell Speed 1 rests on the printed text and the upstream script's comment.
- Stronghold the Moving Fortress: no ruling names the card; rulings on other cards' one-shot 'ATK becomes' effects favour set-not-add but suggest the variant's final-set is too strong for later modifiers.
- Super Rejuvenation: no ruling on whose Tribute counts when the opponent Tributes your Dragon.
- Toon Mermaid: neither the attack lock after a Normal or Flip Summon nor a Level-based Tribute is addressed; both are edge cases reachable only through effects that override its Summoning rules.
- Toon Summoned Skull: the 'Summoned' versus 'Special Summoned' attack lock is decided only by inference (period rulings put Toon Monsters on the field by Special Summon).
- Vampire Lord: no ruling on the once-per-turn clause and neither script implements it; whether it changes any play is a classification question.
- Witch of the Black Forest: no ruling on repeated use or on a same-turn follow-up; the Deck-verification step is kept in force at both snapshots by Konami's own rulings.
- Wulf, Lightsworn Beast: nothing names the card; whether any card in the pool could Special Summon it by a non-effect route is a pool question the corpus cannot answer.
- Xy Dragon Cannon: whether the era card could return from banishment after a proper Summon is decided only by inference; the 2005 UDE page says 'except', the 2008 and 2009 FAQ pages name only the Extra Deck and the Graveyard, and no ruling names the banished zone. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): decided under rule 2, verdict unchanged, see section 11.**
- Xyz Dragon Cannon: whether the era card could return from banishment after a proper Summon is decided only by inference; the 2005 UDE page says 'except', the 2008 and 2009 FAQ pages name only the Extra Deck and the Graveyard, and no ruling names the banished zone. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): decided under rule 2, verdict unchanged, see section 11.**
- Xz Tank Cannon: whether the era card could return from banishment after a proper Summon is decided only by inference; the 2005 UDE page says 'except', the 2008 and 2009 FAQ pages name only the Extra Deck and the Graveyard, and no ruling names the banished zone. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): decided under rule 2, verdict unchanged, see section 11.**
- Blast Held by a Tribute: no ruling on a Tribute-Set attacker.
- Blaze Accelerator: one FAQ line says the Pyro monster must still be sent if the target is removed in response, another line in the same entry says the effect disappears. If the second governs the remaining difference is also cosmetic. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): confirmed unresolved, neither rule applies, see section 11.**
- Boss Rush: whose destroyed monsters count, face-down monsters and Big Core rest on the printed text alone.
- Elemental HERO Divine Neos: the only ruling is OCG; nothing TCG addresses the three claimed differences.
- Evil HERO Dark Gaia: no ruling on combined versus original ATK or on the position change being mandatory.
- Ido the Supreme Magical Force: that Setting was allowed under 'cannot Summon' rests on the rulebook's rule that a Set monster is not Summoned and a ruling on another card.
- Masked Beast Des Gardius: never-revivable rests on the class rule for 'except' wording, not on a ruling naming the card; who may receive the Mask is unresolved.
- Mustering of the Dark Scorpions: no ruling on the second Don Zaloog.
- Red-Eyes Wyvern: the activation condition, targeting and the B. Chick exclusion rest on the printed text alone.
- Soul Rope: no document names the card. The rulebook's Damage Step limit and its definition of 'destroyed' leave no case in which the era card triggered where the modern one does not; a card-specific Damage Step permission for Soul Rope would change that and the corpus shows none.
- Swap Frog: no ruling on a face-down monster milled for its effect, or on the 'Frog the Jam' exclusion.
- Totem Dragon: the re-activation after a negation rests on the FAQ's class ruling (Treeborn Frog), not an entry for the card.
- Tri-Blaze Accelerator: a failed send or a failed destruction, and targeting, rest on the printed text; the sister card Blaze Accelerator is ruled to target with the same wording.
- Wild Fire: destroying one versus all Blaze cards and Set copies rest on the printed text alone.
- Evil HERO Infernal Prodigy: the FAQ says a second Tribute of the same copy loses the first End Phase draw, so play looks the same either way; that is an inference, not a ruling on the limit.
- Necrovalley: at Edison, Konami's 2009 Raging Battle ruling (Lava Dragon's non-targeting effect does not resolve under Necrovalley) and the FAQ entries for Agido and Exchange of the Spirit say non-targeting Graveyard movers were already negated; the card's own FAQ sentence says only effects that target were. Whether the Konami document outranks it across the 2010 wording change is the owner's call. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): decided under rule 1, see section 11.**
- YZ-Tank Dragon: the FAQ wording on the four contact-Fusion monsters changed between 2005 and 2008-12; whether the two-clause text was already in force at Edison is an inference. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): decided under rule 2, verdict unchanged, see section 11.**
- Anteatereatingant: that the era card was unrevivable rests on the FAQ's class rule for 'except' wording, not on a ruling naming the card.
- Armored Glass: period sources use 'Equip Spell Card' and 'Equip Card' inconsistently for equipped Union monsters; none names this card.
- Dimension Distortion: nothing says whether the era card could take the opponent's banished monster; the restriction comes from an OCG-derived implementation.
- Spirit's Invitation: who chooses the returned monster rests on the rulebook's general rule for 'select'; the shipped text is OCG-derived.

## 9. Where the shipped variant script and a ruling disagree

Points on which a ruling in force contradicts what an Ignis script does, found while reading the differences. None is a claim of an erratum record and none was acted on (Ignis's scripts are not edited and no replacement was written). They are read from the scripts' text, not run in the duel engine.

- Armored Cybern: the variant script destroys the target even when the equipped monster's ATK cannot be reduced, where the card FAQ says the effect then disappears (read from the script, not run).
- Axe of Despair: the variant script (from the undated c0 ruling event, left alone) can return the card even after its trigger missed timing; the card FAQ (after Cyber Jar) says the timing has passed.
- Big Shield Gardna: the variant script checks that the card is face-down only when the Spell is activated; the FAQ lets a Book of Moon chained to the Spell flip it face-down and still negate it (read from the script, not run).
- Blue-Eyes Toon Dragon: at Level 5 the variant script requires one Tribute to be available but pays none; no ruling covers the case.
- Dark Magician of Chaos: the variant script lets Skill Drain negate the leave-field banish; the UDE Skill Drain ruling says that effect is not negated.
- Dark Strike Fighter: the variant script omits the Synchro revive limit; the rulebook and the Crimson Crisis ruling say a Synchro Monster cannot be revived without a proper Synchro Summon.
- Future Fusion: the variant script appears to let contact-Fusion-only monsters be selected, which the card FAQ forbids (read from the script, not run).
- Jirai Gumo: on an odd Life Point total the variant script leaves the controller one point less than the FAQ's rounding rule gives (the FAQ line is under Solemn Judgment, a different card).
- Nutrient Z: the variant script offers the card from the hand; the rulebook says a Trap must be Set first (read from the script, not run).
- Twin-Headed Behemoth: the variant script lets Skill Drain stop the revival's bookkeeping; the FAQ says the revival still happens under Skill Drain.
- Diffusion Wave-Motion: Ignis's script (the modern implementation the list uses) lets the card be activated against an empty field; the FAQ says it cannot be. An implementation gap, not a history question.
- Gaia Soul the Combustible Collective: Ignis's script (the modern implementation) has no once-per-turn limit on the Tribute effect; the FAQ says one. An implementation gap, not a history question.

## 10. What this audit does not establish

- Anything about a card the corpus does not mention: `unresolved` is the outcome and the existing call stands.
- The range in force of a UDE-era ruling beyond the owner's decision: when Konami withdrew the UDE-era card FAQ is unknown (a product decision, `docs/state.md`).
- The OCG-side of any card, and the Japanese first printings.
- Whether Ignis's variant scripts behave as read here: no engine test was written for a script's unchanged behaviour; the two records whose lists moved back to the modern card are tested (`tests/engine/test_shared_historical_scripts.py`).
- Night Assailant: held back by the owner's decision of 2026-09-23. Both period sources rule that both of its effects target; nothing rules on whether the effect was optional (the timing rulings point to mandatory). Recorded, not acted on, and the check names that decision as its `owner_decision`. **Round 038 (2026-09-29): the check's `owner_decision` now records the owner's confirmation of 2026-09-29 instead (section 11).**


## 11. Round 038: the two tie-break rules, round 037's review findings, and the exemptions (2026-09-29)

Round 037 left records unresolved because two period sources disagreed. On 2026-09-29 the owner set how such conflicts are decided (`docs/state.md`, "Period rulings"): **rule 1**, a Konami document current at the snapshot outranks an earlier UDE-era ruling on the same point; **rule 2**, between two versions of the same UDE FAQ, the latest version in force at the snapshot governs. A conflict inside one entry of one document is decided by neither. Every quotation below was re-read at the archived capture named in `data/sources.json` (fetched 2026-09-29 from the Internet Archive's raw captures; PDFs through `pdftotext`, HTML stripped). No suite run is evidence for anything here. The rules decide which of two sources governs; they do not turn silence into a ruling, an OCG ruling into a TCG one, or a Yugipedia line into a source.

### 11.1 The named cases

| case | rule | governing text | outranked text | verdict | action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Necrovalley**, c1 `d1` (Edison) | 1 | Konami, Raging Battle card rulings (compiled 2009-05-27, v1.2): "If "Lava Dragon's" effect is activated while "Necrovalley" is activated, the effect does not resolve." and "Its effect does not target, so the Dragons are chosen at resolution of the effect." | Konami-hosted UDE card FAQ (2008-12-16, L-O), the card's own entry: ""Necrovalley" will NOT negate effects that do not target a card or cards in the Graveyard." | `d1` contradicted at Edison; not applicable at Tengu (c1 is in force there); `d2` unresolved, unchanged | c1 narrowed to `d2`; c0's claim about play at Edison narrowed; gap statements gain a dated withdrawal; coverage unchanged, no list change |
| **YZ-Tank Dragon**, c1 `d1` | 2 | XYZ FUSION MONSTERS entry, Konami-hosted 2008-12-15/16, the 2009-01-16 capture and UDE's own U-Z page of 2009-02-26, word for word: "These monsters cannot be Special Summoned from the Extra Deck except by the method described in their text. [...] They cannot be Special Summoned from the Graveyard even after they have been Special Summoned by the method described in their texts." | 2005 UDE card rulings page: "These monsters cannot be Special Summoned EXCEPT by the way described in their text. They cannot be Special Summoned with "Monster Reborn", "Magical Scientist", etc." | `d1` unresolved at Edison and Tengu, unchanged | none on the transition; both wordings recorded in the check, dated notes |
| **XY-Dragon Cannon**, c1 `d1`; **XYZ-Dragon Cannon**, c2 `d1`; **XZ-Tank Cannon**, c1 `d1` | 2 | as above (one shared entry for all four cards) | as above | `d1` unresolved at Edison and Tengu, unchanged | as above |
| **Blaze Accelerator**, `d3` | neither | (none) | (none) | `d3` unresolved (period sources conflict), unchanged | none; the record is not touched |

**Necrovalley.** The Konami document is a registered `konami-document`. Its archived file is byte-identical (SHA-1 `bb6b25f8d4b80332bbf9c03767799a2b93c55ebb`) at the Internet Archive captures of 2009-07-10, 2010-02-15 and 2011-01-02, so it was being served before and after Edison's snapshot (2010-04-24). Lava Dragon's effect does not target and Special Summons a Dragon from the Graveyard: it is `d1`'s case (an effect that moves another card out of a Graveyard without targeting it). The FAQ's sentence is identical in the 2005 page and in every 2008-12 and 2009 copy read, so rule 2 has nothing to decide, and the FAQ disagrees with itself: its own Agido entry (A-C: ""Agido"'s effect does not target. While "Necrovalley" is active, the effect of "Agido" will activate, but will be negated.") and Exchange of the Spirit entry (D-E: ""Necrovalley" will negate the effect of "Exchange of the Spirit". "Exchange of the Spirit" does not target cards in the Graveyard, so "Big Burn" cannot be activated.") say what the Konami document says. Konami's Absolute Powerforce rulings (compiled 2010-02-05, also current at Edison) mention Necrovalley only for Gravekeeper's Stele, whose text says it cannot be negated by it; the errata lists and rulebook 7.1 do not name the card. What this does not establish: whether the TU02 reprint (2010-01-09, the printed "target" wording) changed how Konami ruled. No document read says so, and a printed text is not itself a ruling on play; the owner may want to confirm that reading. An effect that moves its own card (Treeborn Frog's) is not decided by the ruling. `d2` (effects that touch a card in a Graveyard without targeting or moving it) stays `unresolved`: the FAQ's general sentence bears on it only by inference, and it is outranked in part. c0's transition (the printed 'affect' to 'target' change) is left functional; whether anything of it is left at Edison is not re-audited.

**The four contact-Fusion cards.** Rule 2 makes the 2008-12 wording (and UDE's own 2009 page, the latest version in the corpus) govern over the 2005 page. What it says: the Extra Deck route is barred except by the card's own method, and the Graveyard is barred even after a proper Summon. What it does not say: anything about the banished zone. The 2005 wording said "EXCEPT" but listed Monster Reborn and Magical Scientist (Graveyard and Extra Deck) as its examples, so it was not a ruling on banishment either. The Dimension Distortion sentence ("When a "Special Summon-only" monster is removed, without returning to the hand or Deck, after it is Special Summoned properly, it can be Special Summoned by "Dimension Distortion".") is identical in the 2005 page and the 2008-12 pages, so rule 2 does not decide it; it speaks of "Special Summon-only" monsters generally (the FAQ's own examples are Ritual Monsters and Chaos Emperor Dragon - Envoy of the End, in the Dimension Fusion entry beside it), and names none of the four. Reading it as covering them is an inference, and inference is not a ruling. The verdict stays `unresolved`, and the transitions, coverage and lists are unchanged.

**Blaze Accelerator.** One FAQ entry (A-C, 2008-12-16; the 2008-12-31 capture reads the same) says "If an effect is chained that removes the targeted monster from your opponent's side of the field, you must still send the Pyro-Type Monster to the Graveyard." and, a few lines later, "If you activate "Blaze Accelerator's" effect and an effect is chained that removes the selected target from the field, "Blaze Accelerator's" effect disappears, but you still cannot declare an attack during the turn." That is a conflict inside one entry of one document, and the copies do not differ, so neither rule applies. The record is left as round 037 left it (`d3` unresolved, a tracked `erratum.contradicting-ruling-range-unresolved` warning).

### 11.2 The search for other cases

Two searches, both over the corpus of 55 archived documents this round re-fetched (the 2005 UDE page, the Konami-hosted card FAQ copies of 2008-12-15, -16, -17, -20, -31 and 2009-01-16, -19, UDE's own U-Z page of 2009-02-26, the rulebooks, errata lists and per-set rulings).

1. **Section 5, mechanically.** Each of its 219 differences was read for a `supports` and a `contradicts` finding together, or an `unresolved` verdict that carries a `contradicts` finding. Nine hits: Ancient Fairy Dragon `d2` (two Konami documents, the Ancient Prophecy rulings and the rulebook; decided in round 037), My Body as a Shield `d2` (a Konami errata list and the UDE FAQ, which agree that the era card answered monster effects), Toon Summoned Skull `d1` and Evil HERO Infernal Prodigy `d1` (an inference from one source, no second source), Necrovalley, XY-, XYZ- and XZ- and Blaze Accelerator (above). No other record has a Konami document and an earlier UDE-era ruling disagreeing on a claimed difference.
2. **The FAQ entries, version against version.** Every entry (1,620 headings) was compared across all copies. 105 read differently in at least two. For the 93 records round 037 changed, each differing entry that is the card's own or is cited in its check was read against the claimed differences. The points that differ, and why none is a case: **Rescue Cat** (2005: you can activate with fewer than two Level 3 or lower Beasts in the Deck and Summon none; 2008-12: you cannot activate with one or none) and **Heavy Mech Support Platform** (2008-12 adds that the Limiter Removal doubling ends when the platform is destroyed instead) and **Vampire Lord**, **Royal Oppression**, **Divine Wrath**, **Relinquished**, **Bottomless Trap Hole**, **Jam Breeding Machine**, **Deck Devastation Virus**, **Dark Magician of Chaos** (2008-12 adds that it is not removed from play if destroyed in the hand or Deck) and **Tyrant Dragon** (2005 carries a Scapegoat replay example the 2008-12 copy lacks) differ on points none of the records claims; **D.D. Survivor** (2008-12 adds the once-per-turn and next-End-Phase lines) is the entry round 037 already used, which rule 2 makes the governing version; **Skill Drain**, **Fiber Jar**, **Horus the Black Flame Dragon LV8**, **Magical Scientist**, **Kuriboh**, **Dark Paladin**, **Dark Balter the Terrible**, **Summoner of Illusions** and **Ultimate Baseball Kid** differ in terminology only ("Fusion Deck" against "Extra Deck", "Multi-Trigger Effect" against "Quick Effect"), except that the 2008-12 Skill Drain entry adds a Kaiser Seahorse line the records do not use. The one entry that differs on a claimed difference is the XYZ FUSION MONSTERS entry, above.

The search is over the documents in the corpus; a passage in a document not in it is not searched, and a difference the automatic comparison cannot see (a ruling that lives in a different entry from the card's own) can be missed. The list of cases is therefore: Necrovalley, the four contact-Fusion records and Blaze Accelerator, and no others found.

### 11.3 What else changed (round 038's parts B and C)

- **B1.** Section 8's first sentence corrected (above).
- **B2.** The Edison and Tengu `notes` fields state no count; a test (`tests/test_format_notes_state_no_counts.py`) keeps one from coming back. The Tengu note's pool count (4,562) had drifted from the pinned 4563 in the same silent way and was removed with the rest.
- **B3.** Night Assailant's `owner_decision` records the owner's confirmation of 2026-09-29 (held back; the evidence: both effects target, whether the effect was optional is unresolved) instead of the Builder's wording. Nothing is acted on.
- **B4.** Dark Necrofear's `c2` is narrowed to the face-up restriction (the FAQ says the era equip selected 1 target) and carries a full `rulings_check`. The rulebook's general rule ("1 face-up monster of your choice" for Equip Spell Cards, which a Monster Card treated as an Equip Card follows) points the same way; it is not a ruling on the card and is not counted, so the face-up point is `unresolved (general rule only)`.
- **C.** Dark Necrofear `c2`, Fushioh Richie and Second Coin Toss carry full checks, and `ERRATUM_RULINGS_GATE_EXEMPT` and its code path are deleted.
